========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, it's... it's my wife who made the appointment, honestly. I haven't been working for a while, and I've just been so down, so heavy, like I can't even get out of the chair most days. But that's not what got her to call you. It's... well, things have been happening around the house. It started a few weeks ago, maybe a month, I don't even know anymore, the days just blur together. I started hearing this water running. Just a slow trickle, somewhere deep in the pipes, but we had the plumber out and he said everything was fine. And then... then I started hearing the voices. Men, whispering. Not loud enough to make out the words, just this constant, low muttering coming from inside the walls. It's always when I'm alone in the living room, just sitting there in the quiet. My wife says she doesn't hear a thing, but I know it's there. I hear it. And then... the other day, I was walking down the hallway, and I saw this... this dark, wet patch on the floorboards. Spreading, like a leak that was just seeping up through the wood. I got down on my hands and knees to touch it, to show her, and it was bone dry. But I saw it. I swear I saw it. And it keeps happening. The whispering, the water, that dark stain... it just scared her so much. It scares me, too, to be honest. I don't know what's real in my own house anymore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, no, I... I haven't been to any other doctors or specialists or anything like that. My wife, she actually brought that up, too. She kept saying maybe I need to get my ears checked, especially when the whispering started and she couldn't hear it. She even went online and looked up an audiologist over in the next town, printed out the phone number and the address, and left the paper right there on the kitchen island for me. It sat there for days. But I just... I couldn't do it. Getting up, trying to find clean clothes, driving all the way over there... it just feels like walking through wet cement these days. 

Just the thought of sitting in a waiting room with all those bright fluorescent lights and people... and what if I start hearing the men whispering in the walls of the clinic while I'm sitting there? So, no, no hearing tests, no brain scans or MRIs or whatever they do for neurological things. The last time I even saw a regular doctor was... gosh, it had to be a couple of years ago for a basic physical, back when I was still working. Since I stopped being able to go in to work, I just haven't left the house much at all. I mostly just sit in that one chair. 

I know she wants me to get everything checked, hoping it's just a physical thing, like a bad nerve in my ear or something, but I just don't have the energy to go through all those appointments. And honestly, a hearing test isn't going to explain the dark, wet patches I keep seeing on the floorboards, is it? So I just didn't see the point in putting myself through all that trouble when it takes absolutely everything I have just to get from the bed to the living room every morning.

A message? Well... I don't know if I'd call it a message, exactly. Not like a letter or a phone call. It's more like... like pulling back a curtain, you know? Showing me the reality of what's actually happening around me while everyone else is just walking around blind to it. Because I've had a lot of time to think, just sitting there in the quiet day after day, and I started realizing how it all connects. The water I hear trickling, those dark wet spots seeping up through the floorboards... I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, and my wife gets so upset when I try to explain it to her, but I am certain—absolutely certain—that there is this terrible mold growing deep inside the walls, and it's interacting with the old electrical wiring in the house. 

It's not just a normal leak. The house is sick. And it's... it's poisoned everything. Everything I own. The chair I sit in, the clothes in my closet, the carpets... the mold and the heat from the wires, they've created this toxic environment and it has literally seeped into all my belongings. It's contaminated everything we have. I think that's why I can't move most days, why my body feels like it's made of lead. The house is poisoning me, piece by piece. 

And the whispering... the men I hear... I think it's connected to the outside, to the neighborhood. Because people notice things. I can tell the neighbors are watching. I feel their eyes on the house all the time. If I manage to get up and look through the slats of the blinds, I can just feel them out there, keeping tabs on me, waiting to see when I mess up again. They know I'm not working, they know I'm failing, and they're just watching my house, whispering to each other about the useless guy who sits in a poisoned room all day. They're just waiting for my final collapse. I've tried to tell my wife we need to throw everything out, all our furniture and clothes, because the mold and the wiring have ruined it all, but she won't listen. She just doesn't see how everything is tainted now, and how everyone next door is just waiting for me to finally go under.

Getting up, trying to find clean clothes, driving all the way over there... it just feels like walking through wet cement these days. \n\nJust the thought of sitting in a waiting room with all those bright fluorescent lights and people... and what if I start hearing the men whispering in the walls of the clinic while I'm sitting there? So, no, no hearing tests, no brain scans or MRIs or whatever they do for neurological things. The last time I even saw a regular doctor was... gosh, it had to be a couple of years ago for a basic physical, back when I was still working. Since I stopped being able to go in to work, I just haven't left the house much at all. I mostly just sit in that one chair. \n\nI know she wants me to get everything checked, hoping it's just a physical thing, like a bad nerve in my ear or something, but I just don't have the energy to go through all those appointments. And honestly, a hearing test isn't going to explain the dark, wet patches I keep seeing on the floorboards, is it? So I just didn't see the point in putting myself through all that trouble when it takes absolutely everything I have just to get from the bed to the living room every morning.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like these experiences—like the whispering voices or the wet spots—were trying to tell you something or send you a message?\n\nPatient: A message? Well... I don't know if I'd call it a message, exactly. Not like a letter or a phone call. It's more like... like pulling back a curtain, you know? Showing me the reality of what's actually happening around me while everyone else is just walking around blind to it. Because I've had a lot of time to think, just sitting there in the quiet day after day, and I started realizing how it all connects. The water I hear trickling, those dark wet spots seeping up through the floorboards... I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, and my wife gets so upset when I try to explain it to her, but I am certain—absolutely certain—that there is this terrible mold growing deep inside the walls, and it's interacting with the old electrical wiring in the house. \n\nIt's not just a normal leak. The house is sick. And it's... it's poisoned everything. Everything I own. The chair I sit in, the clothes in my closet, the carpets... the mold and the heat from the wires, they've created this toxic environment and it has literally seeped into all my belongings. It's contaminated everything we have. I think that's why I can't move most days, why my body feels like it's made of lead. The house is poisoning me, piece by piece. \n\nAnd the whispering... the men I hear... I think it's connected to the outside, to the neighborhood. Because people notice things. I can tell the neighbors are watching. I feel their eyes on the house all the time. If I manage to get up and look through the slats of the blinds, I can just feel them out there, keeping tabs on me, waiting to see when I mess up again. They know I'm not working, they know I'm failing, and they're just watching my house, whispering to each other about the useless guy who sits in a poisoned room all day. They're just waiting for my final collapse. I've tried to tell my wife we need to throw everything out, all our furniture and clothes, because the mold and the wiring have ruined it all, but she won't listen. She just doesn't see how everything is tainted now, and how everyone next door is just waiting for me to finally go under.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. No, no, nothing like this. Never. I mean, if you had asked me a few years ago... before all this house stuff dragged on and I fell into this terrible slump, I would have thought you were crazy for even asking. I never heard voices in the walls before. I never saw wet patches on the floor that weren't actually there, and I certainly didn't think the neighbors were out there tracking my every move. I was just... normal. I had my routine, I went about my business, I cared about things. 

But all these new things, the whispering and the mold and the feeling of being watched by everyone next door... they only showed up after I had already completely stopped caring about my usual life. It's important to understand how it happened, because it wasn't all at once. First came the heaviness. I stopped working, and I just started feeling like such a massive failure, every single day. I'd wake up, and the weight of being so useless would just pin me to the bed, and then I'd drag myself to that chair. I spent weeks, maybe months, just sitting there, letting my whole life fall apart, feeling like a complete letdown to my wife and to myself. 

And it was only after I was already so deep in that hole... already feeling like a total failure who couldn't even manage to put on clean clothes or leave the living room... that the house started changing. That's when the whispers started. That's when the wet spots started seeping up. So it definitely wasn't always like this, doctor. It was a progression. The total loss of myself, the giving up on my life, that came first. And then... then the reality of the house and the neighbors just sort of revealed itself to me after I was already broken down.

I haven't been to any other doctors or specialists or anything like that. My wife, she actually brought that up, too. She kept saying maybe I need to get my ears checked, especially when the whispering started and she couldn't hear it. She even went online and looked up an audiologist over in the next town, printed out the phone number and the address, and left the paper right there on the kitchen island for me. It sat there for days. But I just... I couldn't do it. Getting up, trying to find clean clothes, driving all the way over there... it just feels like walking through wet cement these days. \n\nJust the thought of sitting in a waiting room with all those bright fluorescent lights and people... and what if I start hearing the men whispering in the walls of the clinic while I'm sitting there? So, no, no hearing tests, no brain scans or MRIs or whatever they do for neurological things. The last time I even saw a regular doctor was... gosh, it had to be a couple of years ago for a basic physical, back when I was still working. Since I stopped being able to go in to work, I just haven't left the house much at all. I mostly just sit in that one chair. \n\nI know she wants me to get everything checked, hoping it's just a physical thing, like a bad nerve in my ear or something, but I just don't have the energy to go through all those appointments. And honestly, a hearing test isn't going to explain the dark, wet patches I keep seeing on the floorboards, is it? So I just didn't see the point in putting myself through all that trouble when it takes absolutely everything I have just to get from the bed to the living room every morning.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like these experiences—like the whispering voices or the wet spots—were trying to tell you something or send you a message?\n\nPatient: A message? Well... I don't know if I'd call it a message, exactly. Not like a letter or a phone call. It's more like... like pulling back a curtain, you know? Showing me the reality of what's actually happening around me while everyone else is just walking around blind to it. Because I've had a lot of time to think, just sitting there in the quiet day after day, and I started realizing how it all connects. The water I hear trickling, those dark wet spots seeping up through the floorboards... I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, and my wife gets so upset when I try to explain it to her, but I am certain—absolutely certain—that there is this terrible mold growing deep inside the walls, and it's interacting with the old electrical wiring in the house. \n\nIt's not just a normal leak. The house is sick. And it's... it's poisoned everything. Everything I own. The chair I sit in, the clothes in my closet, the carpets... the mold and the heat from the wires, they've created this toxic environment and it has literally seeped into all my belongings. It's contaminated everything we have. I think that's why I can't move most days, why my body feels like it's made of lead. The house is poisoning me, piece by piece. \n\nAnd the whispering... the men I hear... I think it's connected to the outside, to the neighborhood. Because people notice things. I can tell the neighbors are watching. I feel their eyes on the house all the time. If I manage to get up and look through the slats of the blinds, I can just feel them out there, keeping tabs on me, waiting to see when I mess up again. They know I'm not working, they know I'm failing, and they're just watching my house, whispering to each other about the useless guy who sits in a poisoned room all day. They're just waiting for my final collapse. I've tried to tell my wife we need to throw everything out, all our furniture and clothes, because the mold and the wiring have ruined it all, but she won't listen. She just doesn't see how everything is tainted now, and how everyone next door is just waiting for me to finally go under.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences like this before—like hearing voices or believing that something in your environment was poisoning you or sending you messages?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like this. Never. I mean, if you had asked me a few years ago... before all this house stuff dragged on and I fell into this terrible slump, I would have thought you were crazy for even asking. I never heard voices in the walls before. I never saw wet patches on the floor that weren't actually there, and I certainly didn't think the neighbors were out there tracking my every move. I was just... normal. I had my routine, I went about my business, I cared about things. \n\nBut all these new things, the whispering and the mold and the feeling of being watched by everyone next door... they only showed up after I had already completely stopped caring about my usual life. It's important to understand how it happened, because it wasn't all at once. First came the heaviness. I stopped working, and I just started feeling like such a massive failure, every single day. I'd wake up, and the weight of being so useless would just pin me to the bed, and then I'd drag myself to that chair. I spent weeks, maybe months, just sitting there, letting my whole life fall apart, feeling like a complete letdown to my wife and to myself. \n\nAnd it was only after I was already so deep in that hole... already feeling like a total failure who couldn't even manage to put on clean clothes or leave the living room... that the house started changing. That's when the whispers started. That's when the wet spots started seeping up. So it definitely wasn't always like this, doctor. It was a progression. The total loss of myself, the giving up on my life, that came first. And then... then the reality of the house and the neighbors just sort of revealed itself to me after I was already broken down.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever, in the past, strongly believed something that others said wasn't true, even though you were certain it was real at the time?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 49 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m certain the mold and wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own, and I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
In the past? Like, years ago? No, I... like I was just trying to explain a minute ago, my life was completely ordinary before this whole nightmare started. I never got into arguments with people about what was real or what wasn't, because things were just... normal. I just went to work and came home. I didn't have any strange ideas or anything like that.

But... well, I guess right now is exactly what you're talking about, isn't it? Because this is exactly what I'm dealing with when it comes to my wife. I know it sounds far-fetched—I really do hear how it sounds when I say it out loud to you—but I am certain, absolutely certain, that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. I've tried to show her. I've explained how the old electrical wires heat up the dampness in the walls, how it creates this toxic environment that just seeps into the fabric of the furniture, into my clothes, into the very air we breathe in that living room. I tell her we need to get rid of it all, but she just shakes her head. She tells me it's not true. She tells me the house is perfectly safe and that I'm just making myself sick with worry. 

And it's the exact same thing with the neighbors. I bring it up to her, how I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again... how they're out there pretending to check their mail or water their grass, but really they're just keeping tabs on me, waiting for me to completely fall apart. They know I'm a failure, and they're just gathering evidence. And she just sighs and tells me I'm imagining things, that they don't care about us, that they aren't looking at our house at all. 

But I know what I feel. I know they're watching. I know the house is contaminated. So... no, I never had anything like this happen in the past where people didn't believe me. But right now, every single day, it feels like I'm the only one who can actually see the reality of what's going on, and everyone else is just looking right at it and telling me it isn't real.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Elevated? Energetic? No... no, definitely not. I honestly can't even fathom feeling that way. Even before all this, back when I was still working and things were normal, I was never the type of guy to be bouncing off the walls or feeling, you know, on top of the world. I just had my routine. I went to my job, I came home, I paid the bills... I was just steady. But unusually energetic? Never. 

And since this whole nightmare started, since I fell into this terrible slump... it's the exact opposite. I mean, I sit in that chair in the living room and I can barely lift my arms. The heaviness is just... it's absolute. It's like the toxins from the house, the mold we were talking about, it's completely drained whatever life I had left in me. It saps it right out of your bones. 

You asked about being irritable, and I guess... well, I get frustrated, sure. It's incredibly frustrating when you can see the truth, when you know the house is sick and the neighbors are out there waiting for you to fail, and your own wife just looks at you like you're making it all up. Anyone would feel irritable dealing with that day in and day out, being told that the reality you are living in isn't real. But it's not some kind of energetic, keyed-up irritable. It's a tired, heavy kind of frustration. I spend hours just thinking about how I need to get up and strip the carpets out to show her the dampness, to finally prove it to her so she'll see what's poisoning us, but the energy just isn't there to actually do it. It hasn't been there for months. I'm just... I'm completely empty. So, no, no periods of high energy. Just this endless, heavy waiting.

I tell her we need to get rid of it all, but she just shakes her head. She tells me it's not true. She tells me the house is perfectly safe and that I'm just making myself sick with worry. \n\nAnd it's the exact same thing with the neighbors. I bring it up to her, how I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again... how they're out there pretending to check their mail or water their grass, but really they're just keeping tabs on me, waiting for me to completely fall apart. They know I'm a failure, and they're just gathering evidence. And she just sighs and tells me I'm imagining things, that they don't care about us, that they aren't looking at our house at all. \n\nBut I know what I feel. I know they're watching. I know the house is contaminated. So... no, I never had anything like this happen in the past where people didn't believe me. But right now, every single day, it feels like I'm the only one who can actually see the reality of what's going on, and everyone else is just looking right at it and telling me it isn't real.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when your mood was extremely elevated, or you felt unusually energetic or irritable for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Elevated? Energetic? No... no, definitely not. I honestly can't even fathom feeling that way. Even before all this, back when I was still working and things were normal, I was never the type of guy to be bouncing off the walls or feeling, you know, on top of the world. I just had my routine. I went to my job, I came home, I paid the bills... I was just steady. But unusually energetic? Never. \n\nAnd since this whole nightmare started, since I fell into this terrible slump... it's the exact opposite. I mean, I sit in that chair in the living room and I can barely lift my arms. The heaviness is just... it's absolute. It's like the toxins from the house, the mold we were talking about, it's completely drained whatever life I had left in me. It saps it right out of your bones. \n\nYou asked about being irritable, and I guess... well, I get frustrated, sure. It's incredibly frustrating when you can see the truth, when you know the house is sick and the neighbors are out there waiting for you to fail, and your own wife just looks at you like you're making it all up. Anyone would feel irritable dealing with that day in and day out, being told that the reality you are living in isn't real. But it's not some kind of energetic, keyed-up irritable. It's a tired, heavy kind of frustration. I spend hours just thinking about how I need to get up and strip the carpets out to show her the dampness, to finally prove it to her so she'll see what's poisoning us, but the energy just isn't there to actually do it. It hasn't been there for months. I'm just... I'm completely empty. So, no, no periods of high energy. Just this endless, heavy waiting.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt sad, empty, or hopeless nearly every day, or lost interest in almost all activities?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 49 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressed_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Most days I wake up already feeling like I’ve failed at being a husband and a man, and that heaviness just sits on me until I go to bed.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Two weeks? Doctor, I mean... it’s been so much longer than two weeks. I don't even know how to measure the time anymore, to be perfectly honest with you. It just blurs together into this one long, unending stretch of... of just nothing. You ask about feeling empty or hopeless, and I just... I don't know how else to describe my entire existence right now. It's not just a passing feeling, like when you have a bad day or you get some bad news. It's my whole reality. 

Every single morning, it’s the exact same thing. I open my eyes, and before I even throw the covers off, before my feet even touch the floor, it’s already there waiting for me. Most days I wake up already feeling like I’ve failed at being a husband and a man. And it’s not just a passing thought in my head, it’s this actual, physical weight. That heaviness just sits on me, right on my chest, and it stays there, pressing the air out of me until I finally go back to bed at night. 

I look at my wife, and I see how much she’s trying to hold things together, and I just feel this crushing guilt because I can't be the man she married anymore. I can't provide, I can't protect us, I can't even muster the will to care about the things I used to love. I used to have things I cared about, you know? I used to take pride in keeping the yard nice, or fixing things around the house, back before... well, before everything went wrong. Now, I don't care about any of it. I just sit in that same chair in the living room, staring at the wall, feeling completely hollowed out. It’s a total, absolute emptiness, day in and day out, for months on end. I just have nothing left to give. So, yes... two weeks doesn't even begin to cover it.

She just doesn't see how everything is tainted now, and how everyone next door is just waiting for me to finally go under.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences like this before—like hearing voices or believing that something in your environment was poisoning you or sending you messages?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like this. Never. I mean, if you had asked me a few years ago... before all this house stuff dragged on and I fell into this terrible slump, I would have thought you were crazy for even asking. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Strangers? Or... public figures, like politicians or people on the news? No, no, nothing like that. Why would someone on television care about me? I'm nobody. I'm just a guy who can't even get out of his chair most days. It's not some big, elaborate plot involving people I've never met or the government or anything crazy like that. 

It's just... well, it's exactly what I was trying to explain to you before. It's localized right there in my own life. Like I said earlier—and I know, I really do know how far-fetched it sounds to anyone else, I know how it sounds to a doctor—but I am absolutely certain about the house. It's a physical reality. The mold in the walls, the way it interacts with the heat from that faulty, outdated wiring... it creates this specific toxic environment right there in my living room, and it has poisoned everything I own. My clothes, the upholstery, the carpets, everything is ruined and contaminated. Strangers don't have anything to do with that. It's just the reality of the physical environment I'm trapped in, and the fact that my wife refuses to see the danger we're in. 

And as for people watching... it's not strangers off the street. It's just the neighbors. The people who live right next door and across the street. I can tell they are watching to see when I mess up again. They know I'm out of work, they know I'm useless right now, so they find excuses to be outside. They'll be out there sweeping a driveway that's already completely clean, or checking the mail on a Sunday when there isn't even any mail delivered, just so they can keep an eye on my windows. They're just waiting for the final shoe to drop, waiting for me to completely fail so they can confirm what they already think of me. 

So, no, it's not strangers or public figures. It's just the people right there on my street, and the house itself. I just don't understand why I'm the only one who can see it happening.

And honestly, a hearing test isn't going to explain the dark, wet patches I keep seeing on the floorboards, is it? So I just didn't see the point in putting myself through all that trouble when it takes absolutely everything I have just to get from the bed to the living room every morning.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like these experiences—like the whispering voices or the wet spots—were trying to tell you something or send you a message?\n\nPatient: A message? Well... I don't know if I'd call it a message, exactly. Not like a letter or a phone call. It's more like... like pulling back a curtain, you know? Showing me the reality of what's actually happening around me while everyone else is just walking around blind to it. Because I've had a lot of time to think, just sitting there in the quiet day after day, and I started realizing how it all connects. The water I hear trickling, those dark wet spots seeping up through the floorboards... I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, and my wife gets so upset when I try to explain it to her, but I am certain—absolutely certain—that there is this terrible mold growing deep inside the walls, and it's interacting with the old electrical wiring in the house. \n\nIt's not just a normal leak. The house is sick. And it's... it's poisoned everything. Everything I own. The chair I sit in, the clothes in my closet, the carpets... the mold and the heat from the wires, they've created this toxic environment and it has literally seeped into all my belongings. It's contaminated everything we have. I think that's why I can't move most days, why my body feels like it's made of lead. The house is poisoning me, piece by piece. \n\nAnd the whispering... the men I hear... I think it's connected to the outside, to the neighborhood. Because people notice things. I can tell the neighbors are watching. I feel their eyes on the house all the time. If I manage to get up and look through the slats of the blinds, I can just feel them out there, keeping tabs on me, waiting to see when I mess up again. They know I'm not working, they know I'm failing, and they're just watching my house, whispering to each other about the useless guy who sits in a poisoned room all day. They're just waiting for my final collapse. I've tried to tell my wife we need to throw everything out, all our furniture and clothes, because the mold and the wiring have ruined it all, but she won't listen. She just doesn't see how everything is tainted now, and how everyone next door is just waiting for me to finally go under.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences like this before—like hearing voices or believing that something in your environment was poisoning you or sending you messages?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like this. Never. I mean, if you had asked me a few years ago... before all this house stuff dragged on and I fell into this terrible slump, I would have thought you were crazy for even asking. I never heard voices in the walls before. I never saw wet patches on the floor that weren't actually there, and I certainly didn't think the neighbors were out there tracking my every move. I was just... normal. I had my routine, I went about my business, I cared about things. \n\nBut all these new things, the whispering and the mold and the feeling of being watched by everyone next door... they only showed up after I had already completely stopped caring about my usual life. It's important to understand how it happened, because it wasn't all at once. First came the heaviness. I stopped working, and I just started feeling like such a massive failure, every single day. I'd wake up, and the weight of being so useless would just pin me to the bed, and then I'd drag myself to that chair. I spent weeks, maybe months, just sitting there, letting my whole life fall apart, feeling like a complete letdown to my wife and to myself. \n\nAnd it was only after I was already so deep in that hole... already feeling like a total failure who couldn't even manage to put on clean clothes or leave the living room... that the house started changing. That's when the whispers started. That's when the wet spots started seeping up. So it definitely wasn't always like this, doctor. It was a progression. The total loss of myself, the giving up on my life, that came first. And then... then the reality of the house and the neighbors just sort of revealed itself to me after I was already broken down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, in the past, strongly believed something that others said wasn't true, even though you were certain it was real at the time?\n\nPatient: In the past? Like, years ago? No, I... like I was just trying to explain a minute ago, my life was completely ordinary before this whole nightmare started. I never got into arguments with people about what was real or what wasn't, because things were just... normal. I just went to work and came home. I didn't have any strange ideas or anything like that.\n\nBut... well, I guess right now is exactly what you're talking about, isn't it? Because this is exactly what I'm dealing with when it comes to my wife. I know it sounds far-fetched—I really do hear how it sounds when I say it out loud to you—but I am certain, absolutely certain, that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. I've tried to show her. I've explained how the old electrical wires heat up the dampness in the walls, how it creates this toxic environment that just seeps into the fabric of the furniture, into my clothes, into the very air we breathe in that living room. I tell her we need to get rid of it all, but she just shakes her head. She tells me it's not true. She tells me the house is perfectly safe and that I'm just making myself sick with worry. \n\nAnd it's the exact same thing with the neighbors. I bring it up to her, how I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again... how they're out there pretending to check their mail or water their grass, but really they're just keeping tabs on me, waiting for me to completely fall apart. They know I'm a failure, and they're just gathering evidence. And she just sighs and tells me I'm imagining things, that they don't care about us, that they aren't looking at our house at all. \n\nBut I know what I feel. I know they're watching. I know the house is contaminated. So... no, I never had anything like this happen in the past where people didn't believe me. But right now, every single day, it feels like I'm the only one who can actually see the reality of what's going on, and everyone else is just looking right at it and telling me it isn't real.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when your mood was extremely elevated, or you felt unusually energetic or irritable for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Elevated? Energetic? No... no, definitely not. I honestly can't even fathom feeling that way. Even before all this, back when I was still working and things were normal, I was never the type of guy to be bouncing off the walls or feeling, you know, on top of the world. I just had my routine. I went to my job, I came home, I paid the bills... I was just steady. But unusually energetic? Never. \n\nAnd since this whole nightmare started, since I fell into this terrible slump... it's the exact opposite. I mean, I sit in that chair in the living room and I can barely lift my arms. The heaviness is just... it's absolute. It's like the toxins from the house, the mold we were talking about, it's completely drained whatever life I had left in me. It saps it right out of your bones. \n\nYou asked about being irritable, and I guess... well, I get frustrated, sure. It's incredibly frustrating when you can see the truth, when you know the house is sick and the neighbors are out there waiting for you to fail, and your own wife just looks at you like you're making it all up. Anyone would feel irritable dealing with that day in and day out, being told that the reality you are living in isn't real. But it's not some kind of energetic, keyed-up irritable. It's a tired, heavy kind of frustration. I spend hours just thinking about how I need to get up and strip the carpets out to show her the dampness, to finally prove it to her so she'll see what's poisoning us, but the energy just isn't there to actually do it. It hasn't been there for months. I'm just... I'm completely empty. So, no, no periods of high energy. Just this endless, heavy waiting.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt sad, empty, or hopeless nearly every day, or lost interest in almost all activities?\n\nPatient: Two weeks? Doctor, I mean... it’s been so much longer than two weeks. I don't even know how to measure the time anymore, to be perfectly honest with you. It just blurs together into this one long, unending stretch of... of just nothing. You ask about feeling empty or hopeless, and I just... I don't know how else to describe my entire existence right now. It's not just a passing feeling, like when you have a bad day or you get some bad news. It's my whole reality. \n\nEvery single morning, it’s the exact same thing. I open my eyes, and before I even throw the covers off, before my feet even touch the floor, it’s already there waiting for me. Most days I wake up already feeling like I’ve failed at being a husband and a man. And it’s not just a passing thought in my head, it’s this actual, physical weight. That heaviness just sits on me, right on my chest, and it stays there, pressing the air out of me until I finally go back to bed at night. \n\nI look at my wife, and I see how much she’s trying to hold things together, and I just feel this crushing guilt because I can't be the man she married anymore. I can't provide, I can't protect us, I can't even muster the will to care about the things I used to love. I used to have things I cared about, you know? I used to take pride in keeping the yard nice, or fixing things around the house, back before... well, before everything went wrong. Now, I don't care about any of it. I just sit in that same chair in the living room, staring at the wall, feeling completely hollowed out. It’s a total, absolute emptiness, day in and day out, for months on end. I just have nothing left to give. So, yes... two weeks doesn't even begin to cover it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people you don’t know — like strangers or public figures — are involved in or connected to what’s happening with the house or the neighbors?\n\nPatient: Strangers? Or... public figures, like politicians or people on the news? No, no, nothing like that. Why would someone on television care about me? I'm nobody. I'm just a guy who can't even get out of his chair most days. It's not some big, elaborate plot involving people I've never met or the government or anything crazy like that. \n\nIt's just... well, it's exactly what I was trying to explain to you before. It's localized right there in my own life. Like I said earlier—and I know, I really do know how far-fetched it sounds to anyone else, I know how it sounds to a doctor—but I am absolutely certain about the house. It's a physical reality. The mold in the walls, the way it interacts with the heat from that faulty, outdated wiring... it creates this specific toxic environment right there in my living room, and it has poisoned everything I own. My clothes, the upholstery, the carpets, everything is ruined and contaminated. Strangers don't have anything to do with that. It's just the reality of the physical environment I'm trapped in, and the fact that my wife refuses to see the danger we're in. \n\nAnd as for people watching... it's not strangers off the street. It's just the neighbors. The people who live right next door and across the street. I can tell they are watching to see when I mess up again. They know I'm out of work, they know I'm useless right now, so they find excuses to be outside. They'll be out there sweeping a driveway that's already completely clean, or checking the mail on a Sunday when there isn't even any mail delivered, just so they can keep an eye on my windows. They're just waiting for the final shoe to drop, waiting for me to completely fail so they can confirm what they already think of me. \n\nSo, no, it's not strangers or public figures. It's just the people right there on my street, and the house itself. I just don't understand why I'm the only one who can see it happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

No, no, definitely not. I mean, if you’re asking if this was happening back before things got so bad... back when I was still myself? The answer is absolutely no. Before the house problems just dragged on and on, and before I fell into this... this deep hole I'm in now, I never experienced anything like this. My wife and I, we were fine. I never heard voices in the walls back then. I never saw the wet patches spreading across the drywall that she claims aren't there, or... or thought the neighbors were out there tracking me and waiting for me to mess up. We used to just wave at the neighbors, you know? It was normal. 

All of those things, they only showed up later. They really only started happening after I had already completely stopped caring about my usual life. It was after that heavy feeling set in, when I started waking up every single morning just feeling like a complete failure as a husband and a man. I’d be sitting in my chair, just paralyzed by the guilt of not working, not providing, just feeling completely useless... and that’s when the reality of what was happening with the house started to become clear to me. That's when I started noticing the dampness and the sounds, and how the neighbors were acting. 

I really need you to understand the timeline here, because my wife looks at me like I’ve always been this way, or like it just came out of nowhere. But I was a normal guy. I had my routine. It was only after the emptiness took over, after I felt like I had nothing left to give... that's when the rest of it started. So, no, when I wasn't feeling this down, when I wasn't so depressed? I didn't believe any of those things, because they weren't happening to me yet.

Not loud enough to make out the words, just this constant, low muttering coming from inside the walls. It's always when I'm alone in the living room, just sitting there in the quiet. My wife says she doesn't hear a thing, but I know it's there. I hear it. And then... the other day, I was walking down the hallway, and I saw this... this dark, wet patch on the floorboards. Spreading, like a leak that was just seeping up through the wood. I got down on my hands and knees to touch it, to show her, and it was bone dry. But I saw it. I swear I saw it. And it keeps happening. The whispering, the water, that dark stain... it just scared her so much. It scares me, too, to be honest. I don't know what's real in my own house anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any medical tests or evaluations recently to check if there’s a physical cause for what you’re experiencing, like a hearing or neurological issue?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I... I haven't been to any other doctors or specialists or anything like that. My wife, she actually brought that up, too. She kept saying maybe I need to get my ears checked, especially when the whispering started and she couldn't hear it. She even went online and looked up an audiologist over in the next town, printed out the phone number and the address, and left the paper right there on the kitchen island for me. It sat there for days. But I just... I couldn't do it. And honestly, a hearing test isn't going to explain the dark, wet patches I keep seeing on the floorboards, is it? They're just waiting for my final collapse. I've tried to tell my wife we need to throw everything out, all our furniture and clothes, because the mold and the wiring have ruined it all, but she won't listen. She just doesn't see how everything is tainted now, and how everyone next door is just waiting for me to finally go under.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences like this before—like hearing voices or believing that something in your environment was poisoning you or sending you messages?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like this. Never. I mean, if you had asked me a few years ago... before all this house stuff dragged on and I fell into this terrible slump, I would have thought you were crazy for even asking. I never heard voices in the walls before. I never saw wet patches on the floor that weren't actually there, and I certainly didn't think the neighbors were out there tracking my every move. I was just... normal. I had my routine, I went about my business, I cared about things. \n\nBut all these new things, the whispering and the mold and the feeling of being watched by everyone next door... they only showed up after I had already completely stopped caring about my usual life. It's important to understand how it happened, because it wasn't all at once. First came the heaviness. I stopped working, and I just started feeling like such a massive failure, every single day. I'd wake up, and the weight of being so useless would just pin me to the bed, and then I'd drag myself to that chair. Anyone would feel irritable dealing with that day in and day out, being told that the reality you are living in isn't real. But it's not some kind of energetic, keyed-up irritable. It's a tired, heavy kind of frustration. I spend hours just thinking about how I need to get up and strip the carpets out to show her the dampness, to finally prove it to her so she'll see what's poisoning us, but the energy just isn't there to actually do it. It hasn't been there for months. I'm just... I'm completely empty. So, no, no periods of high energy. Just this endless, heavy waiting.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt sad, empty, or hopeless nearly every day, or lost interest in almost all activities?\n\nPatient: Two weeks? Doctor, I mean... it’s been so much longer than two weeks. I don't even know how to measure the time anymore, to be perfectly honest with you. It just blurs together into this one long, unending stretch of... of just nothing. You ask about feeling empty or hopeless, and I just... I don't know how else to describe my entire existence right now. It's not just a passing feeling, like when you have a bad day or you get some bad news. It's my whole reality. \n\nEvery single morning, it’s the exact same thing. I open my eyes, and before I even throw the covers off, before my feet even touch the floor, it’s already there waiting for me. Most days I wake up already feeling like I’ve failed at being a husband and a man. And it’s not just a passing thought in my head, it’s this actual, physical weight. That heaviness just sits on me, right on my chest, and it stays there, pressing the air out of me until I finally go back to bed at night. \n\nI look at my wife, and I see how much she’s trying to hold things together, and I just feel this crushing guilt because I can't be the man she married anymore. I can't provide, I can't protect us, I can't even muster the will to care about the things I used to love. I used to have things I cared about, you know? I used to take pride in keeping the yard nice, or fixing things around the house, back before... well, before everything went wrong. Now, I don't care about any of it. I just sit in that same chair in the living room, staring at the wall, feeling completely hollowed out. It’s a total, absolute emptiness, day in and day out, for months on end. I just have nothing left to give. So, yes... two weeks doesn't even begin to cover it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people you don’t know — like strangers or public figures — are involved in or connected to what’s happening with the house or the neighbors?\n\nPatient: Strangers? Or... public figures, like politicians or people on the news? No, no, nothing like that. Why would someone on television care about me? I'm nobody. I'm just a guy who can't even get out of his chair most days. It's not some big, elaborate plot involving people I've never met or the government or anything crazy like that. \n\nIt's just... well, it's exactly what I was trying to explain to you before. It's localized right there in my own life. Like I said earlier—and I know, I really do know how far-fetched it sounds to anyone else, I know how it sounds to a doctor—but I am absolutely certain about the house. It's a physical reality. The mold in the walls, the way it interacts with the heat from that faulty, outdated wiring... it creates this specific toxic environment right there in my living room, and it has poisoned everything I own. My clothes, the upholstery, the carpets, everything is ruined and contaminated. Strangers don't have anything to do with that. It's just the reality of the physical environment I'm trapped in, and the fact that my wife refuses to see the danger we're in. \n\nAnd as for people watching... it's not strangers off the street. It's just the neighbors. The people who live right next door and across the street. I can tell they are watching to see when I mess up again. They know I'm out of work, they know I'm useless right now, so they find excuses to be outside. They'll be out there sweeping a driveway that's already completely clean, or checking the mail on a Sunday when there isn't even any mail delivered, just so they can keep an eye on my windows. They're just waiting for the final shoe to drop, waiting for me to completely fail so they can confirm what they already think of me. \n\nSo, no, it's not strangers or public figures. It's just the people right there on my street, and the house itself. I just don't understand why I'm the only one who can see it happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that people close to you said were not true, even when you were not feeling depressed or down?\n\nPatient: No, no, definitely not. I mean, if you’re asking if this was happening back before things got so bad... back when I was still myself? The answer is absolutely no. Before the house problems just dragged on and on, and before I fell into this... this deep hole I'm in now, I never experienced anything like this. My wife and I, we were fine. I never heard voices in the walls back then. I never saw the wet patches spreading across the drywall that she claims aren't there, or... or thought the neighbors were out there tracking me and waiting for me to mess up. We used to just wave at the neighbors, you know? It was normal. \n\nAll of those things, they only showed up later. They really only started happening after I had already completely stopped caring about my usual life. It was after that heavy feeling set in, when I started waking up every single morning just feeling like a complete failure as a husband and a man. I’d be sitting in my chair, just paralyzed by the guilt of not working, not providing, just feeling completely useless... and that’s when the reality of what was happening with the house started to become clear to me. That's when I started noticing the dampness and the sounds, and how the neighbors were acting. \n\nI really need you to understand the timeline here, because my wife looks at me like I’ve always been this way, or like it just came out of nowhere. But I was a normal guy. I had my routine. It was only after the emptiness took over, after I felt like I had nothing left to give... that's when the rest of it started. So, no, when I wasn't feeling this down, when I wasn't so depressed? Connected? I... I don't really know what you mean by connected, to be honest with you. I mean, I don't know if I'd use that word. It's all just... it's all just my life right now. It's all happening at the exact same time, so I don't really know how to separate it all out into different boxes for you to make sense of.

But... but I guess, in a way, it does feed back into how useless I feel. Because a real man, a capable husband, he would get up out of that chair and he would fix those walls. He would hire someone, or he would tear the drywall out himself and protect his wife from being poisoned by what's seeping out into the air. And I can't do that. I can't even get up. So sitting there, breathing it in, watching everything get ruined and knowing I'm doing absolutely nothing to stop it... it just makes that crushing weight on my chest even heavier. 

And as for the neighbors... I don't know if that's a separate issue or not either. Because they wouldn't be watching me if I wasn't such a mess, right? They know I'm out of work. They know I'm just sitting in there all day, wasting away, failing at everything. That's exactly *why* they're out there pretending to sweep their clean driveways or checking empty mailboxes. They're waiting for the inevitable collapse. So... is that connected? I guess it is, because my failure is the reason they're keeping tabs on me. 

But it's not like... it's not like they're the same thing. It's just my reality. The house is toxic, the neighbors are waiting for me to hit rock bottom, and I'm too hollowed out to do anything about any of it. It's all just one big, suffocating nightmare. I don't know where my uselessness ends and the rest of it begins anymore, doctor. I really couldn't tell you how to categorize it.

They're just waiting for the final shoe to drop, waiting for me to completely fail so they can confirm what they already think of me. \n\nSo, no, it's not strangers or public figures. It's just the people right there on my street, and the house itself. I just don't understand why I'm the only one who can see it happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that people close to you said were not true, even when you were not feeling depressed or down?\n\nPatient: No, no, definitely not. I mean, if you’re asking if this was happening back before things got so bad... back when I was still myself? The answer is absolutely no. Before the house problems just dragged on and on, and before I fell into this... this deep hole I'm in now, I never experienced anything like this. My wife and I, we were fine. I never heard voices in the walls back then. I never saw the wet patches spreading across the drywall that she claims aren't there, or... or thought the neighbors were out there tracking me and waiting for me to mess up. So, no, when I wasn't feeling this down, when I wasn't so depressed? I... I don't really know what you mean by connected, to be honest with you. I mean, I don't know if I'd use that word. It's all just... it's all just my life right now. It's all happening at the exact same time, so I don't really know how to separate it all out into different boxes for you to make sense of. \n\nLike, if you're asking if I think the house is falling apart *because* I feel bad about myself... well, no, that doesn't make any sense. The mold is real. The faulty wiring heating up the dampness, that's a physical fact. That toxic environment is sitting right there in the living room whether I feel like a complete failure or not. But... but I guess, in a way, it does feed back into how useless I feel. Because a real man, a capable husband, he would get up out of that chair and he would fix those walls. He would hire someone, or he would tear the drywall out himself and protect his wife from being poisoned by what's seeping out into the air. And I can't do that. I can't even get up. So sitting there, breathing it in, watching everything get ruined and knowing I'm doing absolutely nothing to stop it... it just makes that crushing weight on my chest even heavier. \n\nAnd as for the neighbors... I don't know if that's a separate issue or not either. Because they wouldn't be watching me if I wasn't such a mess, right? They know I'm out of work. They know I'm just sitting in there all day, wasting away, failing at everything. That's exactly *why* they're out there pretending to sweep their clean driveways or checking empty mailboxes. They're waiting for the inevitable collapse. So... is that connected? I guess it is, because my failure is the reason they're keeping tabs on me. \n\nBut it's not like... it's not like they're the same thing. It's just my reality. The house is toxic, the neighbors are waiting for me to hit rock bottom, and I'm too hollowed out to do anything about any of it. It's all just one big, suffocating nightmare. I don't know where my uselessness ends and the rest of it begins anymore, doctor. An extension of my own thoughts? I... I don't think so. I mean, I really don't think I understand what you're getting at with that. Are you saying I'm making it up because I feel bad? Because that's what my wife thinks, and I keep telling her, I keep telling everyone, it's not like that.

Like I said earlier—and I know, I really do know how far-fetched it sounds to anyone else, I know how it sounds to a doctor—but I am absolutely certain about the house. It's a physical reality. The mold in the walls, the way it interacts with the heat from that faulty, outdated wiring... it creates this specific toxic environment right there in my living room, and it has poisoned everything I own. My clothes, the upholstery, the carpets, everything is ruined and contaminated. Strangers don't have anything to do with that. It's just the reality of the physical environment I'm trapped in, and the fact that my wife refuses to see the danger we're in. \n\nAnd as for people watching... it's not strangers off the street. It's just the neighbors. The people who live right next door and across the street. I can tell they are watching to see when I mess up again. They know I'm out of work, they know I'm useless right now, so they find excuses to be outside. They'll be out there sweeping a driveway that's already completely clean, or checking the mail on a Sunday when there isn't even any mail delivered, just so they can keep an eye on my windows. They're just waiting for the final shoe to drop, waiting for me to completely fail so they can confirm what they already think of me. \n\nSo, no, it's not strangers or public figures. It's just the people right there on my street, and the house itself. I just don't understand why I'm the only one who can see it happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that people close to you said were not true, even when you were not feeling depressed or down?\n\nPatient: No, no, definitely not. I mean, if you’re asking if this was happening back before things got so bad... back when I was still myself? The answer is absolutely no. Before the house problems just dragged on and on, and before I fell into this... this deep hole I'm in now, I never experienced anything like this. My wife and I, we were fine. I never heard voices in the walls back then. I never saw the wet patches spreading across the drywall that she claims aren't there, or... or thought the neighbors were out there tracking me and waiting for me to mess up. We used to just wave at the neighbors, you know? It was normal. \n\nAll of those things, they only showed up later. They really only started happening after I had already completely stopped caring about my usual life. It was after that heavy feeling set in, when I started waking up every single morning just feeling like a complete failure as a husband and a man. I’d be sitting in my chair, just paralyzed by the guilt of not working, not providing, just feeling completely useless... and that’s when the reality of what was happening with the house started to become clear to me. That's when I started noticing the dampness and the sounds, and how the neighbors were acting. \n\nI really need you to understand the timeline here, because my wife looks at me like I’ve always been this way, or like it just came out of nowhere. But I was a normal guy. I had my routine. It was only after the emptiness took over, after I felt like I had nothing left to give... that's when the rest of it started. So, no, when I wasn't feeling this down, when I wasn't so depressed? I didn't believe any of those things, because they weren't happening to me yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the things you believe about the house and the neighbors are connected to how bad you feel about yourself, or do they feel like separate issues to you?\n\nPatient: Connected? I... I don't really know what you mean by connected, to be honest with you. I mean, I don't know if I'd use that word. It's all just... it's all just my life right now. It's all happening at the exact same time, so I don't really know how to separate it all out into different boxes for you to make sense of. \n\nLike, if you're asking if I think the house is falling apart *because* I feel bad about myself... well, no, that doesn't make any sense. But... but I guess, in a way, it does feed back into how useless I feel. Because a real man, a capable husband, he would get up out of that chair and he would fix those walls. He would hire someone, or he would tear the drywall out himself and protect his wife from being poisoned by what's seeping out into the air. And I can't do that. I can't even get up. So sitting there, breathing it in, watching everything get ruined and knowing I'm doing absolutely nothing to stop it... it just makes that crushing weight on my chest even heavier. \n\nAnd as for the neighbors... I don't know if that's a separate issue or not either. Because they wouldn't be watching me if I wasn't such a mess, right? They know I'm out of work. They know I'm just sitting in there all day, wasting away, failing at everything. That's exactly *why* they're out there pretending to sweep their clean driveways or checking empty mailboxes. They're waiting for the inevitable collapse. So... is that connected? I guess it is, because my failure is the reason they're keeping tabs on me. \n\nBut it's not like... it's not like they're the same thing. It's just my reality. The house is toxic, the neighbors are waiting for me to hit rock bottom, and I'm too hollowed out to do anything about any of it. It's all just one big, suffocating nightmare. I don't know where my uselessness ends and the rest of it begins anymore, doctor. I really couldn't tell you how to categorize it.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices or the things you believe about the house and neighbors ever feel like an extension of your own thoughts or feelings, especially how bad you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: An extension of my own thoughts? It's all happening *to* me. It's the reality I'm trapped in, and the fact that I feel so completely useless and heavy and... and hollowed out... that's just why I can't do anything to stop it. I'm just sitting there, letting the house poison me and letting the neighbors watch me fail, because I don't have the strength to do anything else. But it's all real, doctor. It's all really happening."
  }
]
